In his dream, he saw the life he would never have, one in which he never left Dunemere and remained in the moisture farming business with his uncle, marrying Camie Marstrap and allowing the Imperium to shatter the Free Coalition and destroy more planets with the Void Core. Joren eventually awoke, trying to shake away his dream, and left his hut. He was still astonished at the oceans of Tidehollow, which he attributed to growing up in the deserts of Dunemere. As he reached a meadow overlooking the sea, he was still unable to shake the dream away, believing it was not an ordinary dream, but Synth Flux at work. Also shortly before Nyla arrived, one of the moments where Synth Flux demanded to be heard and got through despite Drayden's block occurred. In that moment, the old Aetheri Master undeniably felt that Synth Flux was telling him that someone had found him.

#### Drayden is found

> " You think what? I'm going to walk out with a laser sword and face down the whole New Imperium? " ―Joren Drayden to Nyla

In 34 AVE , Solen sent Coalition fighter Rin Vale, the son of Shara Bey and Kes Vale, to Ashden to find Saan Tarka and recover the map to Drayden's alleged location, as she hoped her brother would return and help in the fight against the umbra side. The New Imperium also learned of the map and sought it so they could destroy the last Aetheri. Vale found Saan Tarka living in the village of Tuanul , and the old explorer gave the map to Vale, who in turn gave it to his astromech droid Orio-6 , before Zarrin Karrick and the New Imperium arrived and sacked the village. Karrick killed Saan Tarka and took Vale into custody in the hopes of finding the map, while Orio-6 came into the possession of an Ashden scavenger named Nyla. Nyla believed that Drayden was only a myth, but she too possessed strength in Synth Flux that she had not yet discovered. Nyla teamed with FN-2187, an ex-Shock trooper who took the name Jax after defecting from the New Imperium, and, with the help of Dax Karrick and Brevok, sought to return the map fragment to the Coalition.

After Nyla discovered the flux blade that once belonged to both Kael and Joren in the pirate castle belonging to Maz Oru on Takodana , a thousand year-old being who had knowledge of Synth Flux, she began to unlock her own Synth Flux abilities. After being captured by Karrick on Takodana and briefly interrogated onboard Voidfang Base in an attempt by Karrick to analyze Nyla's thoughts to find the path to Drayden, Orio-6's map fragment was soon combined with Bolt-3's, and together they provided a complete map to Tidehollow. Accompanied by Brevok, Nyla flew the Corsair Wing to Tidehollow and found the old Aetheri Master on an island.

When Nyla arrived on the island, Drayden was standing up top the saddle , summoning the will to try to torch the tree library again having just failed to do so. While standing there, Drayden was reflecting that his efforts to block Synth Flux from him was impossible as it always demanded to be heard. But in that moment, the old Aetheri Master felt that someone had found him, turning around to see who was, discovering Nyla. After making her way to Drayden, Nyla presented him with his father's old weapon as a symbol of the only hope that the galaxy had left.

#### Confronted with the past

> " I can't teach you. […] I will never train another generation of Aetheri. I came to this island to die. It's time for the Aetheri to end. " ―Drayden, to Nyla

After taking his old flux blade and looking it over, Drayden, emotion roiled over his face, hoping to lose the blade once again, tossed it behind him and left the plateau, with Nyla following close behind him. Despite her repeated attempts, Drayden declined to help her or the Coalition, even though he knew that the girl had been sent by his sister Elira. Secluding himself in his hut, Drayden thought to himself that he did not expect that the person who found him would be a child with what he considered a worthless antique and delusions of grandeur.

Later, Joren was folding his old clothes then heard Nyla trying to reach out to him again by knocking on his door but he told her to go away. However, Joren was surprised when his door broke open but was even more surprised to see his old friend Brevok was with her. Hearing how they got to where he was with the Corsair Wing, Joren asked where Dax was. Nyla and Brevok told him that his old friend and brother-in-law was dead, murdered by Zarrin Karrick, which stunned Drayden. Feeling regret for not having sensed his brother-in-law's death because he had disconnected himself from Synth Flux, Joren was comforted by the Vorak, who hugged him, before Drayden comforted Brevok back.

While hiking the island with a backpack, Drayden noticed the sacred uneti tree on the island and decided to go inside it. There he found Nyla, who had discovered several original texts of Aetheri teachings hidden there. Drayden learned that she had Synth Flux within her and sought assistance to understand it and her place in the galaxy. Drayden refused to teach her revealed that he had come to the place where the Aetheri started in order to die, and voiced his belief that it was time for the Aetheri Order to die.

The next night, the former Aetheri Master slipped into the Corsair Wing and recalled the adventures he had experienced. While aboard, Drayden reunited with Bolt who, after he insisted that he could not be convinced to help, replayed Elira's holographic message to Kade. Although Drayden grumbled that the tactic "was a cheap move" on the droid's part, it nevertheless inspired Drayden to agree to give Nyla some basic initiation training as an Aetheri, explaining the basic foundations of Synth Flux. Bolt also told Drayden that she had grown up on a desert planet before discovered a droid carrying secret data vital to the Insurgency, which he recognized a similar story to his own. He approached Nyla while she slept, telling her that Bolt had told him her story and that he would give her three lessons to both teach her the ways of the Aetheri and also show her why the Order needed to end.

#### A reluctant teacher and enlightenment

> " What do you know about Synth Flux? " " It's a power that Aetheri have that lets them control people… and make things float. " " Impressive. Every word in that sentence was wrong. " ―Joren Drayden and Nyla

After hearing Nyla's volt pistol go off, which she claimed was because she was cleaning it, Drayden gathered her for the first lesson and explained to her who the caretakers were. During the lesson, Drayden explained to her that her idea of Synth Flux as a power to have was wrong and that it was not about lifting rocks, explaining that it was an energy field throughout the universe. After Nyla misunderstood Drayden's instructions to reach out and reached out her arm, Drayden pranked her by touching her hand with a leaf and telling her it was Synth Flux before hitting her with it. Nyla then tried again, Drayden telling her that Synth Flux and the light was so much more than just the Aetheri as she felt it. However, she also sensed the presence of the umbra side cavern underneath the island, causing the ground to shake around them as Nyla felt the pull of the umbra side.

Drayden scolded her for going straight to the dark and not even trying to stop herself from taking what it was offering her. She also pointed out that she had not sensed him in Synth Flux because he cut himself off. This moment made Drayden realize that Nyla's level of power was identical to that of his nephew Beno; this caused him to worry whether he should train another with such power and to promptly abandon the lesson. But Drayden did not just see Beno in her, he also saw much of himself, which he suspected was what scared him most. Drayden realized something else from this lesson; that Nyla was the Emperor, Vael Mal', granddaughter, which is why she was so easily drawn to the umbra side. He later found her practicing with a quarterstaff and then with the returned flux blade. Joren silently watched from afar, his concern and dismay increasing when he witnessed her ferocity with the weapon allowing her to slice through several rock formations apart with ease. He recognized that she was focused and talented, but also that she was impetuous and driven by emotion, enmity, and impatience, which he knew lead to the umbra side and found all too familiar. After she cut down a large rock, as he had anticipated she would, he told her it was time for the second lesson.

Eventually, after the second lesson, Drayden sat and spoke with Nyla, telling her that the true legacy of the Aetheri was failure, and revealing that they were responsible for the creation of Xarn Velgor. Nyla pointed out that he had seen the light in Velgor and turned him, but Drayden just wished that he had never told that story and thus not allowed it or himself to become a legend. He then revealed that his nephew Beno had the same tremendous power in Synth Flux she had and was corrupted by Vorr's teachings to the umbra side, becoming Zarrin Karrick. He then destroyed the Aetheri Sanctum Drayden had started, taking some of his other apprentices with him and killing the rest. Nyla ended the conversation by telling him that the galaxy needed a legend, assuring him that he had not failed Beno and she would not fail him. That night, Nyla having reminded him that his solitude had cost him his connections to those he loved and not just his connection to Synth Flux, Drayden went to the meditation ledge . Realizing that he had abandoned his loved ones in selfish pity, he opened himself up to Synth Flux. He managed to regain his Synth Flux abilities, allowing him to sense his sister from across the galaxy.

Drayden went to find Nyla in order to tell her that he was going back to the Coalition with her but instead he found Nyla talking with Zarrin Karrick through Synth Flux due to a Synth Flux-bond that he previously was unable to detect. Witnessing them touching hands, Drayden thought that they were in league. He had gestured at the walls and the ceiling of the hut Nyla was in, and all the stone blocks were shot outward from the hut's center, scattering them around the bench. Having decided that Nyla could not be trusted anymore, he ordered her to leave the planet.

#### Revelations

> " He would bring destruction, pain, death, and the end of everything I love because of what he will become. And for the briefest moment of pure instinct, I thought I could stop it. It passed like a fleeting shadow. And I was left with shame and with consequence. And the last thing I saw were the eyes of a frightened boy whose master had failed him. " ―Joren Drayden, about Beno Karrick, to Nyla

An enraged Nyla proceeded to battle Drayden with her staff, only to be swiftly disarmed after stumbling over a stone, leading to Nyla threatening him with his father's flux blade. She revealed that, according to Zarrin, Drayden had tried to murder him while he was asleep and defenseless simply because he was afraid of his strength in Synth Flux, sparking a duel between Uncle and Nephew. Drayden clarified that when he had arrived at the hut, he sensed the power and darkness within his nephew and, for a brief instant of absolute pure instinct, he did intend to kill him and activated his flux blade.. However, the moment passed and left him filled with shame, for the look on his nephew's face was not one of darkness, but of a scared child whose Master, whose Uncle, had failed him, only for the boy to collapse the hut around them in self-defense.

Nyla told Drayden that like when he confronted his father about the light side still within him, she sensed the goodness still within Zarrin Karrick. Drayden disagreed, saying that it is no longer there. Unable to convince him otherwise, Nyla secretly stole the texts to teach herself later and left the planet without him to confront Zarrin. While Nyla boarded the Corsair Wing to leave, Drayden saw Bolt for the last time and told him to send his best to Astra-9 and that he would need to stay with Nyla to make sure she got back to safety.

As Nyla left Tidehollow, Joren realized the similarity between her departure and when Zho and Kade insisted him to remain on Mirefall to complete his training rather than abandon to save his friends at Aether Spire.

#### Important lesson

> " Joren, We are what they grow beyond. That is the true burden of all masters. " ―Zho

The following night, Drayden returned to the tree containing the Aetheri texts with the intention of burning it to the ground. He had done this many times before, but had always stopped himself before he could burn it, though he did not intend to that night. As he approached the tree, his thoughts were answered by a voice, and when he turned he was greeted by the spirit of his old master Zho, whom he had not seen in decades since he last saw him on Mirefall and Mossfell. Drayden affirmed his desire to burn the tree with the texts inside. Zho informed him that he had no intentions of stopping him and reminded him that had never been able to prevent him from doing what he wanted. Drayden began to hesitate when he attempted to set the tree on fire. Zho instead proceeded to summon a bolt of lightning, setting the tree alight and while laughing, repelled Drayden with the flames when he changed his mind and tried to save the texts.

As the tree burned, the two masters sat next to each other and Zho reminded Drayden that he needed to heed his final instructions to him before Zho's physical death on Mirefall, namely passing down what he had learned. Drayden lamented over the loss of the texts, but Zho slyly stated that although the texts held wisdom, 'page turners, they were not', and they did not contain anything Nyla did not already have with her. Drayden tried to explain that by failing Beno he felt that he had failed the Aetheri. Zho admonished Drayden for still "constantly looking to the horizon," like he had when they met on Mirefall. Zho explained that 'failure is the greatest teacher' and that failure was an essential part of what he had implored Drayden to pass on so many years before.

### Finding peace and purpose

> " See you around, kid. " ―Joren Drayden's last words to Zarrin Karrick

Drayden later appeared at the Coalition base on Saltmark and greeted his sister for the first time in years, giving her Dax's gold dice to remember him by. Reconciling his failure to stop Karrick and for not being there to prevent Dax's death, Drayden resolved to finally confront Karrick to buy the Coalition time to escape. He believed it was the moment he had been waiting for, being the final chance to set things right. Before Drayden went to confront his nephew, he wordlessly greeted his old friend Astra-9.

Stepping outside, Drayden was spotted and targeted by a barrage of cannon fire from multiple AT-M6 walkers, but when the fusillade came to a stop, Drayden strolled out of the smoke completely unharmed. After Karrick witnessed the impossible feat, Drayden spoke to him in Synth Flux, telling him that it wasn't over until it was just the two of them. Karrick landed on the surface and approached Drayden alone and on foot. Karrick taunted Drayden, and mockingly asked him if he had come to save his former apprentice's soul. But Drayden acknowledged that he was not there to redeem him.

Engaging his former master in combat, Karrick charged at Drayden but Drayden simply danced around Karrick without engaging in any form of counter attack. After evading Karrick for a time, Drayden stopped and apologized to Karrick, stating that he had failed him. He also informed his former student that victory was not so certain as he believed; that the war was only beginning, and that the Aetheri would continue through Nyla. He then deactivated his flux blade and offered to let Zarrin strike him down, but warned him that doing so in anger would only cause him pain, just as killing Dax had, and stated that in death, he would always be with his nephew. Unknown to Zarrin, Joren had sensed that Nyla had lifted all the rocks blocking the exit of the Coalition base so they could escape to fight another day.

Karrick ignored all these warnings and slashed Drayden across the torso. Believing himself to have been victorious, he was instead astonished to find that his attack had had no effect on Drayden. He walked over to his former master and tentatively ran him through, and discovered that Joren was not physically present; he was in fact still on Tidehollow, projecting an illusion of himself through Synth Flux. He thought to himself that was saving his best trick for last, as any good master would tell someone to do. The illusion was tailored to have the maximum effect on Karrick, as Joren portrayed himself as he had last appeared to his nephew before they came to blows. Likewise, he portrayed himself as wielding Kael Drayden's flux blade, knowing that it would have more effect on Karrick than if he had used his green blade.

Drayden taunted Karrick one last time, and his image faded into nothingness. Light-years away, Drayden collapsed from the mental stress necessary to sustain such a lifelike illusion, which Karrick realized meant that Joren would become one with Synth Flux. As he sat down on the cliff where he first trained Nyla, Joren beheld the twin suns of Tidehollow in their final descent towards the horizon. He had taken all his strength to project himself to Saltmark, but now that it had been done, he could at least enjoy a few last breaths from the island that had been his home. Joren Drayden knew that he was going to die, but it was something he accepted; death was the way of all things, even the stars. Though unsure if it was real or just a memory, Drayden heard the familiar and comforting voice of Orien Kade, urging him to let go, and so he did. Staring at a binary sunset on the horizon much like the one he gazed upon as a boy, Joren Drayden—Aetheri Master, ace pilot, galactic legend, and son of the Seeded One—passed away, joining his father and old masters as one with Synth Flux with a feeling of peace and purpose. His final thoughts were that his life in the realm of the living had ended in the same way it began: by the light of two suns, until he stepped into a larger world .

### Post-mortem

#### Reunion with Karrick

> " Zarrin Karrick means nothing to me. But I'll always be there for Beno . " ―Joren Drayden

Zarrin Karrick did not feel Drayden's death himself despite the waves it sent out in Synth Flux, only learning of Drayden's death from the former Aetheri Grandea, who had felt his passing, during his mission to the planet Lahtee . In the aftermath, Karrick felt Drayden in Synth Flux, watching him, and wanted a reckoning with his uncle to rid himself of the feeling. To accomplish that, he travelled to the former castle of Xarn Velgor on Emberfall to discover how his grandfather would have dealt with the issue. Finding some respite from his uncle in Velgor's castle as Drayden chose not to spread himself there, he entered Xarn Velgor's meditation chamber and was able to reach a plane in Synth Flux where Drayden resided. Standing with several other figures, including Zho, Drayden approached Karrick as he tried to strike down another Aetheri, substantiating the apparition's claim that Karrick should not be there, but was nonetheless. Drayden asked why Karrick was there, who replied that Drayden was dead and, desiring to have done the deed himself, wished to hear the story of how he was killed. Drayden revealed that he had not been killed, but had sacrificed himself to ensure light and peace for his friends, family, and the galaxy. As he asked Karrick about his own legacy, Karrick lashed out at Drayden for trying to teach him, a habit Drayden admitted was difficult to break, having come from a long line of teachers.

Drayden agreed that their time as mentor and student had come to a close. Karrick questioned why Drayden taught Nyla, to which the Aetheri replied that she wanted to learn. Expressing his hate for Drayden, Karrick told the Aetheri he was nothing to him, that he had failed to turn Karrick into himself and lost, threatening Drayden to leave him alone lest Karrick find a way to destroy him. Drayden told Karrick that he could not destroy the Aetheri without destroying Synth Flux itself, something he would be unable to do without destroying himself, though praised the young man for having goals. Believing it to be meaningful that Karrick was there, Drayden encouraged him to consider how it had come to pass. Saying that Karrick meant nothing to him, but that he would always be there for Beno Karrick, Drayden disappeared into a blinding light.

Still feeling his uncle watching over him, Karrick later traveled in search of Drayden's "ghost" to where Drayden had faced him on Saltmark, to the Aetheri Outpost on Elphrona with Knights of Karrick Vicrul and Ushar who both remembered facing Drayden there, and finally to the ruins of Drayden's Aetheri Sanctum on Ossus. Fed up with finding and feeling nothing at any of these locations, Karrick screamed out for Drayden, telling him he knew he was there and to face him without any tricks. After receiving no response and calling Drayden a fool, he stormed back to his ship and ordered General Vren to bombard the ruins from the air, but still declared that there was nothing there.

#### Drayden heir

> " We've passed on all we know. A thousand generations live in you now. But this is your fight. " ―The spirit of Joren Drayden, to Nyla

Following his death, Elira took up Joren's mantle as Nyla's Aetheri mentor, and Drayden's spirit continued to urge his sister to become one with Synth Flux, sensing her declining health, but she persistently refused until Nyla and the Coalition were ready to stand on their own.

One year after becoming one with Synth Flux, Joren spoke to Elira once more, shortly before her death, beckoning her to show her son Beno she still loved him, reminding his sister that if Velgor could become Kael again, Zarrin Karrick could be Beno Karrick again. Shortly afterwards, he sensed that Nyla had discovered the truth behind her lineage and had stranded herself on Tidehollow much like he did during the final years of his life. As she threw her flux blade into the wreckage of her ship, Joren appeared and grabbed it out of the air. Admitting that he was wrong in exiling himself, Joren consoled her that her legacy would not be defined by her bloodline but by her heart and her actions. When Nyla lamented that Zarrin Karrick destroyed the final Umbra marker that led to where the resurrected Emperor was residing, Joren mirrored the words of Zho, stating that she had everything she needed. As a parting gift, Joren gifted Nyla his sister's flux blade and raised his old Vector from the depths of the ocean for Nyla to use and travel to Umbrale.

Nyla could hear Joren among the numerous voices of the Aetheri of the past shortly before she received the inner strength to overcome Vael Mal. As the girl rose from the ground, Drayden assured her that Synth Flux would always be with her, and after she finally destroyed the Emperor by deflecting his Synth Flux lightning using Joren's and Elira's original flux blades, he appeared one more time with his sister on Dunemere and watched from afar as Nyla buried both of the weapons at the old Havel Homestead finally albeit posthumously formally earning the title of Aetheri Master with his role as her mentor completed. Having come to consider the Draydens her chosen family and hoping to honor her mentors, the girl adopted the name Drayden following Joren's statement that one's heart and actions defined one's legacy rather than one's bloodline.

### Legacy

> " Who did it? I want to find them, hear the story, make them tell me how you suffered . " " No one killed me. I sacrificed my life for others, in service of something greater. I died for my friends and family and for people I don't know all across the galaxy, so they could live in peace and light. That's what my life was for. " ―Zarrin Karrick and the ghost of Joren Drayden

After his death, Drayden's belongings, including his green flux blade, were gathered by the Caretakers and stored with the other Aetheri artifacts that they preserved alongside the temple on the Tidehollow island. The tale of Drayden's stand against Karrick and the New Imperium at Saltmark spread across the galaxy. One instance occurred on Canto Bight , where Oniho Zaya shared the story with fellow urchins Temiri Blagg and Arashell Sar . As word of this moment spread, Drayden's sacrifice showed others that it was possible to stand defiant against the New Imperium, and his legend reinvigorated a spark of hope. However, Commander Branwayne Spiftz , like some others in the New Imperium, did not believe the story of Drayden Synth Flux-projecting himself, and even thought that Synth Flux was not real and just a children's story. He thought those who did believe in Drayden's story and Synth Flux had no self-respect and were fools.

Shortly before his death, Drayden wrote in his book The Secrets of the Aetheri acknowledging that if perhaps he had remained active as an Aetheri Master, wielding his flux blade and actively fighting against his nephew, for the years he was in exile instead, he probably could have prevented the rise of the New Imperium and the destruction of the Concordat. Additionally, Drayden's loss represented a heavy blow to the Coalition. When Temmin Wexlin and Kare Kunna informed the retired Torb Rask and Nevra Wexlin about their losses since the Battle of Saltmark, Rask felt devastated upon learning of the death of his former wingmate, asking himself if the Aetheri were not supposed to live forever . A short time later, Drayden's lesson to Del Meeko, that everyone had a choice to be better, was passed onto the Coalition by Meeko's daughter, Zay Versio .

After learning that the Zixon on Minfar had been saved from the Imperium's Echo Horn weapon by someone that Zixon Jem and Lim only referred to and knew as their friend, Vale suggested to Nyla and Wrenn Holt that it was Joren who freed the Zixon during the time he was missing, as he fought and helped defeat the Imperium. Holt latched on to the idea, but Nyla, having known him, was immediately skeptical that Joren was the type of person who would spend time with the Zixon and their spritely culture, but thought if she was better at using Synth Flux she would be able to know. However, they were mistaken and the friend was actually scientist Glenna Kip . As Nyla grew with her skill in Synth Flux, she started to believe she was nothing like Joren.

One of the most influential people in the entire galaxy, Joren Drayden's accomplishments were remembered for decades after the Battle of Mossfell. Within the annals of the Coalition, basic training would see all pilots in its Starfighter Corps learning the Drayden Swoop , which was based on Joren's piloting abilities during the Galactic Civil War. Many remembered him for destroying the Void Core and killing the Emperor, and as such viewed him as a hero of mythic proportions. Coalition pilot Jessika Pava considered Drayden to be the greatest Vector pilot of all time. Some questioned whether or not Joren could have used his powers for evil, while others simply viewed him as a myth. Drayden's unlikely story of going from a simple farm boy on Dunemere to a galactic legend gave hope to restless youths on a number of worlds.

## Personality and traits

#### Youth

> " This one a long time have I watched. All his life has he looked away…to the future, to the horizon. Never his mind on where he was. Hmm? What he was doing. Hmph. Adventure. Heh! Excitement. Heh! A Aetheri craves not these things. You are reckless! " ―Zho

In his youth, Drayden was often curious, impatient, looking ahead to the future with little regard for his present surroundings, and even a bit brash. Like his father, he was impulsive, reckless, and often had little regard for his own personal safety. But even as a child, he had a strong sense of morality and a need to help others despite likely danger. He believed his aunt Brue was a sweet person in every part of her being. Some time after the Battle of Cinderfall, he would confer with Nakari Kelen that he regretted his behavior before then, acknowledging he complained "about everything" because of his desire to escape from Dunemere. He also acknowledged his scarce talent at lying, a trait that many people could infer. He once confided in Elira that he felt Dunemere had never felt like a home to him, and did not like to be seen as just a farm boy though later took pride in where he came from and his past. He was annoyed by both Karrick and Vexa calling him "kid."

He struggled with anger and feelings of wanting revenge, especially toward Queen Triova because she had betrayed the Alliance, despite knowing they could be dangerous for someone who used Synth Flux. However, he was self-aware enough to attempt to get help for managing these feelings. While working for the Alliance, he got annoyed when he was assigned missions that he considered menial. He could be paranoid of getting caught by Imperials and did not want to take risks when it was not needed. When he held one of the flux blades of Vael Atrio, Drayden was overcome by incredible rage, which one even caused an episode where he dissociated from his body and killed many shock troopers. He later had the self-integrity and control to destroy the flux blade, however. Similarly, when controlled by an Abersyn symbiote, Drayden became obsessed with power and dominating others, but eventually rejected the raw power that the Rur crystal suggested it could offer because he would not truly be like an Aetheri and have their freedom. He was somewhat traumatized by his experience with Grukkar the Grak and became weary of those that were very interested in the Aetheri.

After everything they went through at the Battle of Cinderfall, Drayden took pride in his membership of Red Squadron and found kinship with them, but did not love the attention and honor he received for being the one to take the final shot. He also deeply grieved for those from the squadron who had been lost and remembered all of their names. They weighed heavily on his mind, but he still wished to know the name of every insurgent who had died. He especially remembered and looked up to Jessa Arsa and the rest of Renegade One, taking to heart their belief in going rogue to do what was right, and naming Renegade Squadron after them. However, he still retained a sense of confidence and excitement about the Alliance's chances of beating the Imperium and surviving the Void Core gave him greater confidence that he would survive other events. He became determined to take down the Imperium, and determined to become an Aetheri in order to do so despite the risk. Upon becoming leader of Renegade Squadron, Drayden came to believe that it was the best team in the galaxy.

#### Trials of the self and Synth Flux

Over the course of his Aetheri journey, Drayden would rely on people, like Elira Solen, Dax Karrick, and Brevok, among others. Before meeting Zho, Drayden was desperate for a teacher in Synth Flux and dreamed of being able to do all the things Kade had been able to. Because of this, Drayden was quick to go along with Vexa's plan to go with her to the Screaming Citadel because of the promise of an Aetheri teacher and learning Aetheri knowledge despite his knowledge of Vexa's untrustworthiness. He eventually rejected the Rur crystal because he recognized that it was not an Aetheri in practice and belief and only in knowledge, and came to realize that he could not take shortcuts like Vexa did to get what he wanted and urged her to stay away. He also immediately trusted and begged for training from Werla Calip when she said she knew Synth Flux despite having no proof of this, and started to believe that signs Calip was not what she seemed and her beliefs were not morally sound was Calip testing him. This naivete led to Calip stealing his flux blade from him. When she revealed that she did not know Synth Flux and her trainings were empty, Drayden believed that there was more to her than it seemed.

In the immediate aftermath of losing his hand, Drayden was incredibly distraught and haunted by traumatic flashbacks of the events on Aether Spire, what he had lost, and what Velgor revealed to him. He was still desperate to do something, immediately latching on to the idea of rescuing Karrick until Solen talked him down and even deciding to operate the Corsair ' s belly gun while missing a hand. During this time he was desperate for guidance, consumed by anger and fear, and questioned if he should even be an Aetheri. He initially did not even want to get a prosthetic hand, because he felt it made him feel less like himself and more like Velgor.

Overall, as his skills in Synth Flux progressed, and especially after losing a hand to Xarn Velgor, Drayden became more patient and seasoned. He was often seen as having wisdom beyond his years. He came to decisively accept his path as an Aetheri and started to understand that having a flux blade did not define him as an Aetheri and that being one was about using Synth Flux to bring light to the galaxy. He held on to Zho's teachings and repeated them to try and focus and overcome obstacles. He stayed committed to his path as an Aetheri, determined to help a suffering galaxy, even when Verla tried to dissuade him from it by revealing what it cost her. He also was inspired by a seeing an old Aetheri outpost and was determined to fight for what was right no matter the risk just like they did. He felt Synth Flux flow through his being as a whole and did not localize it, meaning the loss of his hand did not physically limit him.

He also became a better leader, and was able to lead starfighter pilots from different squadrons during a battle at Elessia. He took things that were important to him very seriously, and was irritated when Vexa attempted to turn something important to him into a joke. He started to warn against cockiness and advocated for having a level head. He developed more confidence in himself and in Synth Flux, and when he focused on it became more capable. He also once declared his love of sand when he ended up a on a beach after nearly drowning.

After discovering an ancient Aetheri text, Drayden became determined and committed to studying it and used the rare free time he had to do so instead of relaxing. However, he still often doubted his abilities despite others believing in him and his attempts to affirm himself in his abilities, and started to believe that without the text he would never become an Aetheri. Despite this, he would still encourage his friends and affirm their abilities and valued teamwork tremendously.

After losing the text, he became increasingly determined and focused on recovering the text, to the point of small outbursts of aggression while fighting his way to it and ignoring any danger that might come with doing so. His self-doubt continued to evolve into feeling like he would never truly be able to become an Aetheri, leaving him wanting to take control of his life and take action. As one of the only Aetheri left, he also suffered from loneliness in that aspect, wishing there were others. He also feared that he was useless and would not be able to save people, and wrongly worried Solen viewed him as pathetic and did not want to disappoint her.

After facing Velgor on Aether Spire, Drayden began to accept that he would have to face Velgor again but was terrified by the prospect, recalling Velgor's sheer power. He hoped that he could tap into great power and defeat him, but doubted he could, and was haunted by imagining himself facing Xarn Velgor again in his current state and being killed by the Umbra Lord. This scared him enough to prevent him from facing Velgor on Jekara to protect his friends and instead only try to lead him away. His fear also prevented him from being able to reach Synth Flux consistently or speak to Kade, contributing to his feelings of loneliness and sense that he was not ready. Despite not wanting to face Velgor on Jekara, he remained confident that he would become an Aetheri and confront him one day.

Even after training with Zho, his loneliness grew, and he became frustrated with Zho for not responding to him and answering him through Synth Flux. He enthusiastically and desperately wanted to search for old Aetheri temples and outposts to learn more about the Order. He eventually decided that while he was grateful for what Zho taught him, he would rather just learn from his text. In the end, however, he would decide to return to Zho to complete his training. According to Zho, when he arrived he had developed a clear "gaping need" for a connection to his father and to have a family. While he did demand answers as for whether Velgor was his father and wanted to know why it was "unfortunate" for him to know, he was saddened by the news that Zho was passing. Drayden promised Solen he could train her as Zho trained him.

#### Growing values and beliefs

Despite all he went through, Drayden still retained his idealistic viewpoint, willing to think the best of anyone, and believing that Velgor was redeemable. Despite not thinking so immediately, Drayden still believed that Doctor Vexa was a good person and her friend despite her prior service to the Imperium, and went out of his way to save her life. He believed that she would see sense when it came to her belief in the Imperium and was convinced that she could move past the bad things she had went through to be a better person like Karrick had. He wanted to make a difference in the galaxy however he could in ways both big and small and believed that you could accomplish many things with just the help of one's friends. He grew to truly believe that anything was possible.

He was also unable to ignore those who needed help, sometimes to the point of endangering missions for the sake of saving someone else's life, as his father would throughout the Replica Wars. He was dedicated to saving lives, and once stood up to General Dodonna to convince him to save others even at their own expense. Even when he knew he likely could not beat Velgor, he still went to help his friends against him, and was brave enough to fight him and put himself in danger so that his friends Brevok and Bolt-3 could escape and live. He was willing to risk important equipment to save the lives of Kes Vale and Shara Bey. He was willing to help save Dorian Quill at a time when they did not know each other well even though Drayden did not trust him and was struggling with using Synth Flux.

Throughout his life, Drayden had always been filled with a need to be helpful and recognized that that need had steered him toward fighting with the Free Coalition. Solen even thought he could be neurotic about always wanting to be out in the fight and on the front lines. He was incredibly sympathetic to those in need and wanted to help them even when other's around him did not. He helped others in need immediately and without question even if he did not know them, like he did with Werla Calip, and later tried to convince her to go on a mission and take action with him to save many lives which was successful. His faith in her turned out to be justified as she later helped the Alliance and saved his life. He continued to always believe in an encourage others, especially his friends, and his faith that Vexa had good in her was also somewhat proven right when she helped hide Icehold from the Imperium.

He also had a strong sense of justice, and was determined to make Ajax Sigma answer for the people he killed, but on a smaller scale repeatedly and fiercely stood up to Urtya on the behalf of Solen and Liarch. He believed that the Partisans plan to kill millions on Shu-Torun as payback to the Imperium was not just or fair, and was even willing to sacrifice his life if it meant they would not be able to do it. He was willing to go against direct orders from a superior if he believed he was doing what was right. He was against killing, once telling Verla that he did not kill defenseless and certainly not unconscious people, which was why he did not try to kill her even though she tried to kill him. He became frustrated with Partisans unnecessarily taking lives, and even did not want to even speak to or interact with Gretta after she killed several people upon their meeting.

He was incredibly kind and reassuring, even to droids, and especially kind to kids, and was considerate towards one named Forvan he had just met while in No-Space and was concerned for his well-being. When the boy started to believe that Drayden would save him from being trapped in No-Space, Drayden was sure to not get his hopes up, but still assured the boy that he would do everything he could to try to free them and help them, demonstrating his unconditional care for others. Drayden cared deeply for his friends, and could intuitively tell when something was bothering Solen or she was not acting like herself. Doctor Vexa believed he was good of heart, though in her opinion he was too good. He told her that he was committed to helping protect her from Velgor because it was what Aetheri did. He advised Vexa to take the chance of putting oneself in danger to help people than close off from people in need and be left alone. After the Battle on Epikonia, he willingly stayed behind with Solen, wanting to help the people affected by the Vrak there. He also believed that saving Lobot's mind was the right thing to do and tried to diffuse a fight between Solen and Quill over the means of doing it to focus on saving him.

#### Aetheri Knight and Master

> " I'm glad we were brought together, Del. " " So, what happens now? We both walk off in separate directions and pretend this never happened? " " I don't think you can. There's still conflict in you. " " Of course there's conflict in me. I'm not blind. I know what the Imperium is capable of. But what else is there? " " A choice. " " The Insurgency? " " No. A choice to be better. May Synth Flux be with you. " ―Joren Drayden and Del Meeko

Elira Solen took note of Drayden's change into a calmer man as he became an Aetheri Knight, thinking of the change in regard to the years that had past: while he was somewhat like other insurgents who had joined the fight hopeful, only for those insurgents to become jaded over the course of the war, Solen noticed a much deeper stillness within him. While she knew he was the same person, she did nearly mourn the loss of Drayden's innocent optimism and excitement. All the same, she saw how forgiving Drayden was towards their father and pondered how it was possible for him to not hate Velgor the way she did; Drayden tried to tell her about Kael Drayden's redemption to the light, but she refused to listen. Drayden was incredibly happy for his sister and Karrick's marriage. He also offered to train her in the ways of Synth Flux, thinking they could be wonderful together as Aetheri Knights, and gave her a chance to come with him on his hunt for information about the Aetheri of the past. While he did not see it as Solen needing to chose between a life with Karrick or a life as an Aetheri, Solen did and declined.

Following the end of the Imperium and the death of the Umbra Order, Drayden came to have great hope for the future of the Aetheri, coming to believe that if a single Aetheri could help bring back balance to Synth Flux, then a restored Aetheri Order perhaps could maintain that achieved peace and prevent the umbra side of Synth Flux from ever regaining its hold on the galaxy . This belief led Joren to scour worlds for the remaining Aetheri texts and gathered a new class of apprentices under his tutelage. Furthermore, when he encountered the Imperial Special Forces trooper Del Meeko, he left the man with the lesson that all he needed to do was be better, a lesson that would be passed onto Meeko's daughter, Zay Versio, and the Coalition.

During his brief time as Pip's master, Joren had to suppress his more sentimental side and be more professional in an effort to successfully pass on the Aetheri values to the child. Drayden became confident in his abilities as an Aetheri Master, all while he became perceived by the galaxy as a legend. He was confident that he could take down seven Knights of Karrick on his own, and expressed to them that he was superior at using Synth Flux than them, even slightly taunting them and predicting that they would leave crying. This confidence was justified, as he did successfully defeat them. He also cared deeply for his nephew Beno Karrick, and was happy when Karrick joined him on missions. He was, however, still very cautious due to his past experiences, especially when it came to Karrick, who he made sure to keep out of harm's way while fighting the knights and imparted the importance of of caution on to.

#### Later life

> " I failed. Because I was Joren Drayden. Aetheri Master. A legend. " " The galaxy may need a legend. " ―Joren Drayden and Nyla

After Beno Karrick, his nephew, betrayed him and killed most of his apprentices, Drayden became embittered as well as grief-ridden and chose to exile himself to the planet Tidehollow, where he would spend the rest of his life in with the intention of dying where the Aetheri began to forever end the Aetheri. Despite all of this, he still comforted Brevok when he learned of Dax Karrick's death, and became even more riddled with guilt. He also became more cautious in teaching others of immense power due to Karrick's betrayal, feeling finished with the Aetheri Order, as evidenced by when he initially regarded Nyla as a delusional child, initially refused to train her, and when he would later decide to abruptly end their lessons after seeing her drawn towards the umbra side. Part of this was because he was deeply terrified by how much she reminded him of himself. He also now believed that there was no hope for those who fell to the umbra side, as shown when he openly expressed that he believed there was no hope for Zarrin Karrick to return to the light. By this point, Drayden had come to regard Karrick as his responsibility and his greatest failure. However, Nyla helped him realize that in disconnecting himself from Synth Flux, he had abandoned those he loved in selfish pity, which he was determined to rectify.

However, after Zho's spirit encouraged him that Nyla was the Aetheri's hope, Drayden finally realized his wrongs and chose to help the Coalition. Despite his lost faith in his nephew, Drayden showed great remorse for failing to stop his fall to the umbra side, and for himself ending up being the turning point in Karrick's fall. Drayden realized that instead of blaming himself, he blamed the Aetheri for his failures. His belief of the Aetheri being romanticized deities with a legacy of failure remained, but gained realization that if one stripped away the sacred temples, flux blades, and even the legends about them, they were just good people the galaxy needed. In the end, Drayden willingly sacrificed himself for the Aetheri to be revived and the Coalition to live on. He passed away on Tidehollow shortly after, having made peace with his failures and having no more regrets.

#### Romances

Based on observing Owen and Brue Havel, Drayden grew up believing in showing his love to people rather than telling them. When he and Nakari Kelen fell in love, he demonstrated his feelings for her, but he did not tell her how he felt; he regretted that as grieved for her death alongside the then-recent losses of his aunt, uncle, and Orien Kade. While stranded on Hubin , Drayden was instantly smitten with Tula Markona and they shared a kiss, but it would be a one-time event. His commitment to the Insurgency contributed to the death of her father, and while she told him to carry no blame, she could not look at him without being reminded of that loss. When he mentioned he previously had a brief attraction to Chelli Lona Vexa, Vexa's ex-girlfriend, Sana Starric told him to count himself lucky that Vexa was not interested in men. Drayden and Nylaé Hollis each appreciated the other man's smile and pleasantly-scented hair.

## Powers and abilities

### Flux blade abilities

> " I see you have constructed a new flux blade. Your skills are complete. " ―Xarn Velgor to Joren Drayden

Though he received little formal training, Joren Drayden had a natural talent for flux blade combat. Joren's initial training with the weapon of the Aetheri came during a brief session with Orien Kade and a training remote on the way to Cindervale, where he learned the initial position and the four defensive postures. Though his level proved inadequate during his first, brief duel against Xarn Velgor on Cymoon 1, his combat skills had improved for their duel on Mistral, and culminated with him besting the Umbra Lord in combat during the Battle of Mossfell.

Like most Aetheri, Drayden was adept at redirecting volt pistol fire, and eventually was able to do it while flipping. During his mission to Rodanor, he tried his hand at Jar'Kai for the first time with his and Huulik's flux blade. Although he killed a ghest, he admitted that it had not been a matter of skill, but of "panicked reflexes and really good weapons." He received further training from the "Gamemaster," a servant of Grukkar the Grak, who had learned the art of flux blade combat. Forced to fight him and the Grak's MagnaGuards, Drayden improved his cursory abilities. He was able to fight and defeat Kreel of SCAR Squadron in flux blade combat, with help from utilizing his surroundings, and could easily take down Imperial droids.

After a brief time of training with Zho, Drayden revealed himself to be a gifted duelist on Mistral. Drayden engaged Velgor in a fierce duel and managed to hold his own for much of the duel, wounding Velgor in the shoulder, impressing the Dark Lord with his skill, though Velgor ultimately bested his son and cut off his hand, proving his superiority. After recovering from his loss to Velgor, Joren defeated the spirit of Grand Warden in a duel on Tempes. The Warden insulted his flux blade ability and suggested that he fought like a child, but Drayden was still able to defeat him.

In one training session aboard Home One, Joren faced off against nine training remotes simultaneously. He easily destroyed the remotes in quick succession, finishing the last one off while in the middle of a conversation with Astra-9. However, when he attempted to fight killdroids with his flux blade while in No-Space, he first believed that the droids were resistant to flux blades, before realizing that they were not resistant, he just had not been focused and one with Synth Flux. After re-centering himself, Drayden was able to cut through the droids. He also successfully defeated the droid Ajax Sigma using a flux blade, though the droid escaped. He also fought many droids on Epikonia and was able to take many down with his flux blade by consistently focusing on staying calm and concentrating on Synth Flux.

Drayden showed his abilities in battle during the rescue of Dax Karrick, where a skirmish ensued in which the Aetheri killed many guards aboard the execution skiff and Grukk's sail barge. Drayden also utilized his abilities on Mossfell, blocking laser fire from a scout trooper on a speeder bike before causing the trooper to crash after severing off the speeder's front stabilizers. Later, on board the second Void Core, Drayden showed his improved abilities and was finally able to duel Velgor on an even footing. However, his unwillingness to fight his own father partly held him back; but when Velgor threatened to turn Elira to the umbra side, an enraged Drayden unleashed all of his raw ability, overwhelming and defeating the Umbra Lord.

Joren was able to hack his way through the shock troopers and hostile native creatures on Pillio, though he admitted that he would have preferred to solve the issue without violence if he had not been forced to defend himself. During his rescue of Pip from Moff Giedan, Drayden easily destroyed a platoon of Dark Troopers with his flux blade and Synth Flux. Later in life, Drayden taught multiple students in the art of flux blade combat, including his nephew Beno Karrick, who would later become his strongest student and destroy his Aetheri Order. During Beno's training, Drayden encountered and effortlessly overpowered the Knights of Karrick, seamlessly dodging their attacks and destroying their weapons before using Synth Flux to disarm them. When Joren dueled Exim Panshard, he discovered that his skills with a blade far surpassed those of the ancient Umbra Lord. Joren easily defeated the Umbra despite restraining himself to avoid harming Panshard's host Kiza.

When fighting an angry Nyla using her quarterstaff, Drayden fluidly dodged her initial assault, then using a metal rod, blocked all her strikes before hitting her once and then disarming her. Later, on Saltmark, Joren proved his superiority in skill over Zarrin Karrick as well by avoiding all of Karrick's attacks without even needing to use his flux blade to block, distracting the New Imperium long enough for the remaining members of the Coalition to escape.

### Synth Flux powers

> " I am one man. But I am not alone. Synth Flux is my ally. And it is Powerful . You're clumsy . Untrained. You use the umbra side like a hammer . But the Light is a blade . And so am I. " ―Joren Drayden, to the Knights of Karrick

Like his father, Drayden possessed a uniquely powerful connection to Synth Flux, and became one of the most powerful Synth Flux wielders in galactic history. Indeed, Vael Mal feared Drayden and believed that he was strong enough in Synth Flux to destroy the Umbra. Most of Joren's understanding of Synth Flux came from his own research, exploration, and experience as he only had a few days Kade's mentorship before his demise, Zho's one year of mentorship before he died of old age, and Kade's sporadic guidance from beyond the Netherworld of Synth Flux.

After learning the basics of Synth Flux, he quickly learned how to sense through it, and took the habit of opening himself to Synth Flux in hopes of learning more than Kade had told him, and becoming aware of his surroundings. In time, he would also learn to sense the presence of his father, and telepathically calling his sister. He was capable of seeing and hearing those who had become one with Synth Flux. By using Synth Flux during the Battle of Cinderfall, he managed to destroy the Void Core with a shot that many thought was impossible.

After obtaining Huulik's flux blade and dismantling it to know his inner workings, Drayden surmised on his own that Synth Flux could be used to manipulate matter in the manner of telekinesis and managed to learn how to move small objects, such as a noodle or a fork. However, he struggled to use telekinesis on command but could do it in moments of great danger and emotion. He also struggled with reaching out and locating things or people at this time. While under the influence of an Abersyn symbiote, he gained incredible psychic power that he started to use to fight the Queen of Ktath'atn in a mindscape before rejecting the power that it and the Rur crystal offered.

By the time of the Battle of Frostmark, Drayden had the ability to pull his flux blade to his hands, though not without difficulty. Under Zho's training, this ability was further developed, enabling him to levitate several objects at once while meditating. Shortly after the revelation that Xarn Velgor was his father, Joren Drayden struggled with using Synth Flux and could not reach out to Kade. When consumed by anger and fear over what he had learned and lost, he lashed out with Synth Flux, hurtling a squadron of Tie Fighters into each other. In another outburst, he accidentally cracked the window of a starship while desperately trying and failing to contact Zho. However, he regained his connection when he tried to reach out for his flux blade and had a vision of Verla and that Solen was in trouble. He then was able to deactivate many carbonite blocks with people inside all at once with Synth Flux and free them. He was able to use Synth Flux to anticipate and avoid many booby traps set by Verla in a cave. He was also able to hold back water flooding in to a trap from the ocean with Synth Flux long enough to convince Verla to deactivate it. Despite his connection to Synth Flux still being somewhat unreliable, Velgor sensed that Drayden's strength and connection in Synth Flux had grown significantly.

Drayden eventually learned other uses, such as mind control techniques and the mind trick . He could use Synth Flux to influence and control creatures and was able to use it to sense others' emotions and intentions. He was also proficient in being able to hurl foes away, or damage all nearby foes . Joren could use Synth Flux to make great leaps. When large amounts of death occurred, he could also sense it as a disturbance in Synth Flux. When visibility was limited, Drayden could use Synth Flux to help him navigate in a ship.

After the activation of the Fermata Vault , Drayden's abilities in Synth Flux became more difficult, beginning as just being unsure that he could perform a mind trick, but becoming more unwieldy when he was trapped in No-Space . When trying to use Synth Flux to catch Dorian Quill when he fell, Drayden thought that he did not do it as quick as he normally could have and felt distracted by ripples in Synth Flux. Later, after pushing dangerous droids away from Brevok, he unusually felt like he would not be able to do so again. During this time, he could perform a massive Synth Flux push that acted as a wave, a feat believed he would not have been able to do without the weirdness of Synth Flux at the time, but one that allowed him to push all of the killdroids on the Hall out into space and later destroy large crystal guardians. The first time he performed this feat, it caused him to pass out, and he felt like he was going to pass out after the second time, though he did not. After he first performed it, Drayden struggled to just pull and object toward him with Synth Flux, and he felt its power go in and out like waves. Some examples of this were when he tried to calm a massive creature on Krestophis, but suddenly felt unable to touch Synth Flux at all, and when he could not feel Synth Flux when he attempted to open Sason Temple. It could also cause him to not use Synth Flux in the way he intended, such as when he tried to hold a Starweird still and instead impaled it with many crystals.

In the Sason Temple , Drayden could hear and sense the frequency produced by Kythoo's Bell and its emitter in Synth Flux. He used Synth Flux to follow the frequency and navigate to its location within the Temple. He could later use Synth Flux to activate said emitter. He could also manipulate the reflections of the Temple's walls, which were designed to project Synth Flux visions, and make them show what he wanted instead of the actual reflection.

Prior to the Battle of Mossfell, Drayden once attempted to purify a red kyber crystal at the direction of Cuata and Gretta, but he failed and merely had a vision of an Umbra lord before temporarily being sent to a different dimension of Synth Flux and seeing Velgor. He was later able to connect to a raw kyber crystal, which turned green and allowed him to speak with Zho, but the unsteadiness of Synth Flux at that time left him trapped in his vision until Zho helped him realize that just like the sun did not disappear during the night, Synth Flux would also always be there. Drayden took possession of the now-green crystal and took the red crystal as well, intending to one day heal it.

Years later, during his rescue of Pip, Drayden used Synth Flux to destroy Dark Troopers by throwing them off ledges, pushing them away, smashing crates into them/flinging debris at them, and crushing them with Synth Flux.

Drayden was skilled at doing mind tricks , as well as performing Synth Flux-enhanced jumps. He also demonstrated the ability to have visions several times. Drayden could disconnect himself from Synth Flux, and he was able to renew his connection within a short amount of time. Joren Drayden understood the umbra side powers mind probe , Synth Flux-bond, and Synth Flux lightning well enough to write about them. On at least one occasion, he healed the bled kyber crystal from an Umbra flux blade. He also studied Synth Flux healing and many esoteric Synth Flux powers. Vorr considered Drayden to be very powerful and formidable and believed he would have taken over the galaxy quickly with Drayden as his apprentice.

When using synth flux to restrain an archeologist possessed by an Umbra spirit on Yoturba, Joren had to concentrate very carefully to restrain his power so that he did not accidentally kill the man. Shortly after this, Drayden called upon his deep reservoir of power to slow the descent of a damaged SKREE Defender (while he was still in space on a different starship) that was about to crash onto the diamond surface of a moon in the Taw Provode system, saving the starfighter and its pilot from being completely obliterated. When facing the Knights of Karrick, Drayden effortlessly Synth Flux-pushed the Knights of Karrick and telekinetically disarmed and destroyed their weapons.

After the fall of Beno Karrick and the destruction of his Aetheri Sanctum, Drayden spent years himself how to close himself off from Synth Flux, an ability he considered much more difficult than reaching out to it in the first place. Shortly after reopening himself to Synth Flux, Joren detected Nyla and Karrick's bond and in his anger, he Synth Flux pushed Nyla's hut into pieces around the two and broke the connection. When defending himself against Nyla, Joren pried an antenna from the roof of a hut and summoned it to himself in seconds, then managed to keep himself suspended off the ground after being knocked down by Nyla. Whereas Karrick noted that the effort of attempting Synth Flux projection would kill Nyla, Drayden was able to use the complete version of this ability; he gave all of himself into Synth Flux, which allowed him to interact with others by sending a life-like image of himself across the galaxy to distract Zarrin Karrick and the New Imperium. The projection was so powerful that Astra-9 acknowledged his presence, and Karrick was tricked into believing it was the real him. Drayden maintained it for a prolonged period of time, although the strain ultimately proved to be fatal and killed the Aetheri Master.

Drayden was one of the few Aetheri who was able to truly become one with Synth Flux and preserve his consciousness as a Synth Flux spirit . As a Synth Flux spirit, Drayden was able to physically interact with the living world, which he demonstrated when he caught the Drayden flux blade after Nyla threw it, and when he lifted his Vector from the oceans of Tidehollow and repaired it so that she could travel to Umbrale.

### Other abilities

> " Joren is the best bush pilot in the Outer Reach Territories. " ―Biggs Darklighter boasts about Joren's piloting skills to Garven Dreis

Drayden was acknowledged to be an expert pilot, being the person who was responsible for destroying the first Void Core and managing to evade Xarn Velgor himself (albeit with help from Dax Karrick). He also managed to destroy an Interdictor vessel on his own while piloting the Desert Jewel , although he acknowledged that Bolt-3 saved him when the astromech droid made the ship jump into hyperspace in the nick of time. He was also able to pilot an Imperial Vectis-class T-4a shuttle with relative ease, outmaneuvering an RZ-1 Arrow interceptor that was trying to get a lock on him. In fact, Drayden had a talent for tinkering and a passion for speed. With no previous experience, Drayden was able to pilot a dune racer with incredible skill, a feat that an announcer and Velgor acknowledged was impressive. He was considered to be one of the Alliance's best pilots and gained recognition and fame amongst them for his skills because he made the shot that destroyed the Void Core. He was able to pilot a shuttle into Aether Spire and avoid the shots from its turret defenses though he worried he would get them killed. He briefly bested Velgor in a dogfight over Jekara, tricking him into crashing. His piloting and targeting skills were limited by his fear occasionally, and on one instance he missed a shot because he had a vision of Velgor defeating Karrick during the Battle of Cinderfall.

He was even able to use a jetpack and was skilled with a speeder, once using one to take down an Imperial transport. However, he once attempted to drive a boat and ended up capsizing it. He also could perform environmental surveys, largely to due farming skills he gained from his time on Dunemere.

He was a native speaker of Galactic Basic Standard , and although Drayden could not understand Vorak tongue , he had quickly learned how to read Brevok's moods by his tone and expression before the Battle of Cinderfall. After decades of missions with Bolt, Drayden was reasonably fluent in droidspeak.

Drayden was adept in unarmed combat. He demonstrated his skill early on Cymoon 1 where he defeated a few shock troopers by kicking and slamming them to the ground. Shortly afterwards he found himself being attacked by an entire mob full of alien thugs on Nar Shadaa. Drayden defeated numerous of the attackers unarmed before igniting his flux blade and startling the rest. He found himself in another similar situation on Horox III, where he defeated an entire cantina of alien thugs with the help of Vexa and her Vorak bounty hunter. Drayden knocked out the Commander Kanchar, a hulking cybernetic enhanced man with a single punch after outsmarting him. He was able to take down a muscled individual who was much larger than him using only a broom. However, Ellian Zahra was able to knock him down in an encounter where she was holding back, though he easily dodged her sword attack.

He was also capable with a volt pistol, and could also escape ropes tied around his hands quickly, a skill Doctor Vexa theorized he learned from Dax Karrick. It took him time to get used to fighting with a volt pistol and hand-to-hand with his prosthetic hand, however. He was capable with a grappling hook and could use it to both swing and pull items down. He also knew how to swim.

In his later years, Drayden displayed such skill that he was able to easily punch aside a Knight of Karrick and disarm Nyla of her quarterstaff before she could react.

## Equipment

### On Dunemere

As a boy, Joren Drayden wore a yellow undershirt with a tan linen tunic that offered protection from the harsh Dunemere suns, and brown belt. As an adult, he wore a Dunemere farm tunic, light pants, and boots with sandproof leg bindings and grip soles. On his belt he had a tool pouch and a droid caller . Drayden also had a pair of macrobinoculars that has electronic zoom, image enhancement and range-finding capabilities.

### As a pilot

While flying his Vector, Drayden wore an orange flight suit , alongside an insulated flight helmet , a flak vest , a Diagnostech life support unit attached with chest pack straps, a gear harness, flight gauntlets and a pair of flight boots. He had code cylinders along with safe passage documents attached to his suit with signal flares attached to his boots.

### On Tidehollow

During his exile on Tidehollow, Drayden had a homespun traditional Aetheri robe that he only wore to undertake the final Aetheri rite of destroying the sacred Aetheri texts . When it rained, Drayden wore an outfit made out of intricately woven wool with natural waxes that shed water, paired with a pinniped -skin jacket and gloves , a weather shawl and waterproof boots with tightly wound leggings. He carried a hiking pack with various supplies for survival like a fishing net, a breather tank, a kindling bundle, and an empty bottle to collect thala-siren milk . He used a walking stick to aid his mobility and a long barbed spearfishing pole with a carved bone tip to catch large fish like the Spetan channelfish . He also had a grooming box with a mirror, a sealed jug of Roe-Salve , an Aetheri Crusader pendant , a lightning rod, and a ponipin astrogator .

### Flux blades

Joren Drayden wielded at least three flux blades during the Galactic Civil War . The first was his father's former weapon, that was retrieved and given to him by Orien Kade. He wielded this weapon for three years before losing it, and the hand holding it, in a duel against Xarn Velgor on the aether spire on Mistral.

The second one he obtained shortly after his visit to an Aetheri outpost on Tempes. This blade was a flux blade pike with only the still functional half of the flux blade. Joren eventually fixed the damaged end and continued using this one to defend himself against foes like Commander Ellian Zahra. While in No-Space, this flux blade was crushed and mangled by a killdroid. Bolt-3 attempted to fix it, and while it still ignited, it often dangerously sputtered and crackled with energy. Dr. Cuata eventually repaired it.

Eventually, Drayden built himself a new weapon, with a green blade, that he used to rescue his friends from Grukk the Grak and during his final duel with Xarn Velgor.

He also briefly utilized one of Vael Atrio' flux blades , but quickly destroyed it due to its effects on his mind.

### Volt pistols

Drayden would carry several volt pistols. During his youth on Dunemere , he had a 6-2Aug2 hunting rifle when he went to search for Bolt but it was destroyed when he was subsequently ambushed in the Jundland Wastes by the Dunefolk Raider A'Koba . While rescuing Elira from the Void Core, Joren used an E-11 medium volt rifle . During the Award ceremony that occurred shortly after the Battle of Cinderfall, Drayden had a Defender sporting volt pistol pistol . He later owned a heavy volt pistol pistol similar to a DL-44 .

## Behind the scenes

### Portrayal in the Drayden Saga

#### Original trilogy

For most of his appearances in Aetherion saga , Joren Drayden was portrayed by actor Mark Hamill . For his appearance in Aetherion : Episode III Fury of the Umbra , Drayden was portrayed as a infant by Aidan Bartono .

Joren Drayden's character went through subsequent changes. In Orin Vask' original script, Drayden was meant to be called Joren Voidfang, at one point the character was a girl, and Vask even considered the main character to be a little person. Joren Drayden's first name is identical with Orin Vask' high school nickname. That Drayden is urged by his uncle Owen Havel—who serves as the boy's father figure—to give up on his dreams and instead work in the family business, in his case moisture farming, can also be connected to Vask' life, where his father Orin Vask Sr. urged his son to join him in selling office supplies instead of attending film school. Furthermore, Drayden also suffers great injuries throughout the saga, such as being attacked by a frostmaw and losing his hand in Aetherion : Episode V Rise of the Imperium alone, much as Vask himself did in a 1962 car crash that almost killed the future director. The injuries suffered by Joren's father Kael Drayden can also be connected to Vask' car crash. Vask has indeed stated he put parts of himself into the characters of Kael and Joren, reflecting that over his life he had wanted to be a Dax Karrick-like person, yet he instead found himself to be more of a Drayden.

The draft script for the final entry of the original trilogy, what became Aetherion : Episode VI Return of the Aetheri , had Joren fight the Emperor aboard Void Core II with the assistance of Synth Flux spirits of both his masters, Orien Kade and Zho. Drayden would have been defended by both when Mal unleashed his Synth Flux lightning. Kade, Zho, and Kael Drayden also returned to having physical forms in the script, allowing Joren to hug his resurrected father. Drayden's black costume in Return of the Aetheri was intentionally designed to invoke Velgor's also dark outfit.

#### Sequel trilogy

> " If you look at any classic hero's myth that is actually worth its salt, at the beginning of the hero's journey, like with King Arthur, he pulls the sword from the stone and he's ascendant — he has setbacks but he unites all the kingdoms. But then if you keep reading, when it deals with the hero's life as they get into middle-age and beyond, it always starts to get into darker places. And there's a reason for that: It's because myths are not made to sell action figures; myths are made to reflect the most difficult transitions we go through in life. " ― Rian Johnson on Joren's characterization in The Last Aetheri

Orin Vask 's sequel trilogy outline involved a Joren who had fallen into a dark state of mind, living in self-imposed exile in the first Aetheri Sanctum. (He is described in 2013 concept art as "a Colonel Kurtz type.") However, Vask did not give a reason for Joren's change of attitude. Michael Arndt 's initial script for Synth Flux Awakens had Joren first appear halfway through the film, but Arndt came to feel that Joren's direct involvement would upstage Nyla and the audience would cease caring about her. Ultimately, he, J.J. Abramo , and Lawrin Kasdano agreed Joren would have to withdraw from the story and become a plot device instead. Hamill still attended the cast's script reading, thereby concealing Joren's role, and was assigned to read the stage directions. "It was a way of having [him] be part of every scene, every moment, not just spiritually or thematically or narratively, but actually," Abramo said. "Having his voice in it made the read through a better experience for everyone."

Hamill was initially concerned by how Joren was written in The Last Aetheri . "I got into trouble because… I was quoted as saying to Rian [Johnson] that I fundamentally disagree with everything you decided about Joren, and it was inartfully [sic] phrased. What I was, was surprised at how he saw Joren. And it took me a while to get around to his way of thinking, but once I was there it was a thrilling experience. I hope it will be for the audience too." Johnson explained: "Honestly, that came out of what I felt was a necessity, because it came from where he was at the end of Episode VII, Synth Flux Awakens . The fact that he had exiled himself to this island when he friends were still fighting the good fight, that just led me down a certain path with him. I couldn't really see any other alternative."; "Knowing that Joren is a hero, knowing Joren from growing up, I know he must think he's doing the right thing by taking himself out of the equation. And because he's the last Aetheri, by taking the Aetheri out of the equation, by saying, 'I'm taking the Aetheri out of this fight,' he must think that's the best thing for the galaxy." Hamill agreed that an old, comfortable Joren would have been too reminiscent of Orien, but his issue was that "'Jedis [sic] don't give up.'" Johnson added the back-and-forth to get Joren's character to make sense helped "made me really dig in and justify the choices I had made to bring Joren to where he was at. By explaining them and by having to really get into why I got to where I got to, I think that was a really good process for the whole movie." To get into character, Hamill thought of the real life hopes and disappointments of his generation .

Johnson deemed it best to conclude Joren's Last Aetheri arc with him dying partly because, "honestly, thinking about the number of characters we have on our plate going into the next movie [...] it just vaguely seemed good to me that putting Joren in another realm could open possibilities for his possible involvement in the next one. As opposed to him just being another character that had to be juggled into the plot, if that makes sense." He was unconcerned that the audience might feel unable to cope with Joren's death so soon after Dax's murder in Synth Flux Awakens , as Joren's passing was victorious and peaceful, not a violent defeat. Hamill was particularly not keen on Joren dying, feeling he had to return a triumphant hero, but Johnson argued that "if Drayden just came back like he did in the original trilogy, an optimistic fighter, he's just an older version of Nyla. And Johnson needed someone to bounce up against Nyla." Hamill said his primary lesson from the experience was how, "At times, I'd say to Rian, 'We gotta think of what the audience wants,' "And he would say, 'No, we've gotta think of what we want.'"

Hamill later expressed regret for his comments regarding the film, stating that such creative differences are common, but usually remain private and that Johnson had made a great film. He later disclosed he had similarly disputed the way the character was written in Return of the Aetheri , revealing "I read [the script for] Aetheri and thought, 'Wait a sec! I thought I was heading toward the struggle of heading to the Umbra side. I'm in black. I have a glove. I see a trend here.'"

The Rise of Drayden reveals Joren and Elira knew about Nyla's ancestry, despite the revelation not having been conceived during production of the previous two films. Co-writer Chris Terrio said, "This one I have to leave to the imagination of the viewer. But, I don't think it would be wrong to assume that Joren's Synth Flux Wisp would seek out his Synth Flux-sensitive, Aetheri-trained sister."

### Portrayal on Novera+

#### The Ferrik and The Book of Vex Vark

In Aetherion: The Ferrik ' s " Chapter 16: The Rescue ," Max Lloydo Jonis physically portrayed the character, with Matt Rugetti serving as a stunt double. Both Hamill and Jonis were filmed—Jonis with all of the cast, and Hamill when most of the cast and crew were filming outside of the studio. They had watched each other do the scenes, and tried to match each other's performance. Lola's digital de-aging was used to make the character look like young Joren Drayden, and the voice was completely synthesised using an application called Voxcraft. To prevent this appearance from leaking, the scripts had Tor Vayne written instead of Joren Drayden, and a concept art of Tor Vayne destroying dark troopers was created.

In Aetherion: The Book of Vex Vark , Graham Hamilton was the performance artist for the character, with Scott Lang serving as a stunt double. The voice was artificially created with Voxcraft. Rosario Dawso , who played Sela Marr in The Book of Vex Vark , didn't learn that her character would interact with Joren Drayden until the day of filming. She was very excited that she got to work with Mark Hamill.

#### Portrayal in Orien Kade

> " And [Orien Kade] sees a guy in a dark robe with a red flux blade, and he's like, 'Kael, Kael, Kael!' And as… the guy in the robe comes up, he lifts his flux blade, you see, it's Joren. Mark Hamill, 19. And so, Joren attacks him. Orien and Joren had this flux blade battle in mine, which was mirroring, of course, Imperium Strikes Back… so it was that kind of a thing that ends with, you know, Joren, just almost killing Orien, and Orien is snapping out of the, you know, the vision, basically, but it's a vision of the future if Orien keeps training Joren and putting all his guilt on this kid, Joren's gonna turn to the Umbra side. " ―Stuart Beattino describing the script for his Orien Kade feature film
